The U.S. Nuclear Posture Review made public in 2002 specifically envisioned the use of nuclear weapons on a first strike basis, even against non-nuclear armed states.[\[192\]](#cite%5Fnote-194) Investigative reporter Seymour Hersh reported in 2006 that the Bush administration had been planning the use of nuclear weapons against Iran.[\[193\]](#cite%5Fnote-195) When specifically questioned about the potential use of nuclear weapons against Iran, President Bush claimed that "All options were on the table." According to the _[Bulletin of the Atomic Scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulletin%5Fof%5Fthe%5FAtomic%5FScientists "Bulletin of the Atomic Scientists")_, "the president of the United States directly threatened Iran with a preemptive nuclear strike. It is hard to read his reply in any other way."[\[194\]](#cite%5Fnote-196)
* In September 2007, [Condoleezza Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condoleezza%5FRice "Condoleezza Rice"), [U.S. Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.%5FSecretary%5Fof%5FState "U.S. Secretary of State"), cautioned the IAEA not to interfere with international diplomacy over Iran's alleged weapons program. She said the IAEA's role should be limited to carrying out inspections and offering a "clear declaration and clear reporting on what the Iranians are doing; whether and when and if they are living up to the agreements they have signed." Former IAEA Director General ElBaradei called for less emphasis on additional UN sanctions and more emphasis on enhanced cooperation between the IAEA and Tehran. Iran has agreed with IAEA requests to answer unresolved questions about its nuclear program. ElBaradei often criticized what he called "war mongering," only to be told by Rice to mind his business.[\[195\]](#cite%5Fnote-197)
* In December 2007, the United States [National Intelligence Estimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National%5FIntelligence%5FEstimate "National Intelligence Estimate") (which represents the consensus view of all 16 American spy agencies) concluded, with a "high level of confidence", that Iran had halted all of its nuclear weapons program in 2003 and that the program remains frozen. The new estimate says that the enrichment program could still provide Iran with enough raw material to produce a nuclear weapon sometime by the middle of next decade but that intelligence agencies "do not know whether it currently intends to develop nuclear weapons" at some future date. Senator [Harry Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry%5FReid "Harry Reid"), the [majority leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party%5Fleaders%5Fof%5Fthe%5FUnited%5FStates%5FSenate "Party leaders of the United States Senate"), said he hoped the administration would "appropriately adjust its rhetoric and policy".[\[51\]](#cite%5Fnote-autogenerated3-53)[\[52\]](#cite%5Fnote-autogenerated2-54)
* On 2 February 2009, the thirtieth anniversary of the Islamic Revolution in Iran, Iran launched its first domestically produced satellite.[\[196\]](#cite%5Fnote-Reuters-198)[\[197\]](#cite%5Fnote-individual.com-199) Iran's President Mahmoud Ahmadinejad described the successful launching of the Omid data-processing satellite as a very big source of pride for Iran and said the project improved Iran's status in the world.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] The United States claimed Iran's activities could be linked to the development of a military nuclear capability and that the activities were of "great concern".[\[198\]](#cite%5Fnote-200) The U.S. specifically said it would continue "to address the threats posed by Iran, including those related to its missile and nuclear programs."[\[199\]](#cite%5Fnote-201) Despite the U.S. saying it would use all elements of its national power to deal with Tehran's actions,[\[200\]](#cite%5Fnote-202) Iran said the launch was a step to remove the scientific monopoly certain world countries are trying to impose on the world.[\[201\]](#cite%5Fnote-203) Iraqi National Security Advisor Muwafaq al-Rubaie said Iraq was very pleased with the launch of Iran's peaceful data-processing national satellite.[\[202\]](#cite%5Fnote-204)
* In March 2009, [Richard N. Haass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard%5FN.%5FHaass "Richard N. Haass"), President of the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council%5Fon%5FForeign%5FRelations "Council on Foreign Relations"), wrote that U.S. policy must be thoroughly multilateral and suggested recognizing Iranian enrichment while getting Iran to agree to limits on its enrichment. "In return, some of the current sanctions in place would be suspended. In addition, Iran should be offered assured access to adequate supplies of nuclear fuel for the purpose of producing electricity. Normalization of political ties could be part of the equation," Haass said.[\[203\]](#cite%5Fnote-205) In October 2009, [Ploughshares Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ploughshares%5FFund "Ploughshares Fund") President Joseph Cirincione outlined "five persistent myths about Iran's nuclear program": that Iran is on the verge of developing a nuclear weapon, that a military strike would knock out Iran's program, that "we can cripple Iran with sanctions", that a new government in Iran would abandon the nuclear program, and that Iran is the main nuclear threat in the Middle East.[\[204\]](#cite%5Fnote-206)
* In 2009, Independent U.S. Security Consultant [Linton F. Brooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linton%5FBrooks "Linton Brooks") wrote that in an ideal future "Iran has abandoned its plans for nuclear weapons due to consistent international pressure under joint U.S.–Russian leadership. Iran has implemented the Additional Protocol and developed commercial nuclear power under strict International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA) safeguards using a fuel leasing approach with fuel supplied by Russia and spent fuel returned to Russia."[\[205\]](#cite%5Fnote-207)
* A 2009 [U.S. congressional research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional%5FResearch%5FService "Congressional Research Service") paper says [U.S. intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5FIntelligence%5FCommunity "United States Intelligence Community") believes Iran ended "nuclear weapon design and weaponization work" in 2003.[\[206\]](#cite%5Fnote-CRS09-208) The intelligence consensus was affirmed by leaders of the U.S. intelligence community.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Some advisors within the Obama administration reaffirmed the intelligence conclusions,[\[207\]](#cite%5Fnote-209) while other "top advisers" in the Obama administration "say they no longer believe the key finding of the [2007 National Intelligence Estimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5Fprogram%5Fof%5FIran#2007%5FIran%5FNational%5FIntelligence%5FEstimate "Nuclear program of Iran")".[\[208\]](#cite%5Fnote-210) [Thomas Fingar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas%5FFingar "Thomas Fingar"), former chairman of the National Intelligence Council until December 2008, said that the original 2007 National Intelligence Estimate on Iran "became contentious, in part, because the White House instructed the Intelligence Community to release an unclassified version of the report's key judgments but declined to take responsibility for ordering its release."[\[209\]](#cite%5Fnote-211)
* Lieutenant General Ronald Burgess, the chief of the Defense Intelligence Agency, said in January 2010 that there is no evidence that Iran has made a decision to build a nuclear weapon and that the key findings of a 2007 National Intelligence Estimate are all still correct.[\[210\]](#cite%5Fnote-212)
* On 20 July 2011, [Frederick Fleitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederick%5FFleitz "Frederick Fleitz"), a former CIA analyst and House Intelligence Committee staff member, took issue with a February 2011 revision of the 2007 [National Intelligence Estimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National%5FIntelligence%5FEstimate "National Intelligence Estimate") on Iran's nuclear weapons program in a Wall Street Journal op-ed titled "[America's Intelligence Denial on Iran](https://www.wsj.com/articles/SB10001424052702303661904576453800512114910)." In the op-ed, Fleitz claimed the new estimate had serious problems and underplayed the threat from Iran's pursuit of nuclear weapons program much as the 2007 version did. However, Fleitz stated that he was not permitted by CIA censors to discuss his specific concerns about the estimate. Fleitz also claimed the estimate had a four-member outside review board that he viewed as biased since three of the reviewers held the same ideological and political views and two of them were from the same Washington DC think tank. He noted that the CIA prevented him from releasing the names of the outside reviewers of the 2011 Iran estimate.
* Several high U.S. military and intelligence officials have stated that the effects of an Israeli attack on Iran's nuclear facilities would not be preventive. Defense Secretary [Leon E. Panetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon%5FE.%5FPanetta "Leon E. Panetta") said in December 2011, and Lt. Gen. [James R. Clapper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James%5FR.%5FClapper "James R. Clapper"), Director of National Intelligence, said in February 2012 that an Israeli attack would only delay Iran's program by one or two years. General [Michael V. Hayden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael%5FV.%5FHayden "Michael V. Hayden"), former CIA Director, said in January 2012 that Israel was not able to inflict significant damage on Iran's nuclear sites. He said, "They only have the ability to make this worse."[\[211\]](#cite%5Fnote-LATimes120216-213) In February 2012, Admiral [William J. Fallon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William%5FJ.%5FFallon "William J. Fallon"), who retired in 2008 as head of [U.S. Central Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.%5FCentral%5FCommand "U.S. Central Command"), said, "No one that I'm aware of thinks that there's any real positive outcome of a military strike or some kind of conflict." He advocated negotiating with Iran and deterring Iran from aggressive actions and said, "Let's not precipitate something."[\[212\]](#cite%5Fnote-CSPAN230212-214)[\[213\]](#cite%5Fnote-CSIStranscript-215) General [Martin Dempsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin%5FDempsey "Martin Dempsey"), Chairman of the [Joint Chiefs of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint%5FChiefs%5Fof%5FStaff "Joint Chiefs of Staff"), said in August 2012 that a unilateral Israeli attack on Iran would delay but not destroy Iran's nuclear program and that he did not wish to be "complicit" in such an attack. He also stated that sanctions were having an effect and should be given time to work, and that a premature attack might damage the 'international coalition' against Iran.[\[214\]](#cite%5Fnote-JTA0-216) Former Defense Secretary and former CIA Director [Robert Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert%5FGates "Robert Gates") stated in October 2012 that sanctions were beginning to have an effect and that "the results of an American or Israeli military strike on Iran could, in my view, prove catastrophic, haunting us for generations in that part of the world."[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-VApilot0-217)
* In 2011, the senior officers of all of the major American intelligence agencies stated that there was no conclusive evidence that Iran has made any attempt to produce nuclear weapons since 2003.[\[216\]](#cite%5Fnote-LATimesFeb2013-218)
* In January 2012, U.S. [Defense Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense%5FSecretary "Defense Secretary") [Leon Panetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon%5FPanetta "Leon Panetta") stated that Iran was pursuing a nuclear weapons capability, but was not attempting to produce nuclear weapons.[\[16\]](#cite%5Fnote-autogenerated4-16)
* In 2012, sixteen United States intelligence agencies, including the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA "CIA"), reported that Iran was pursuing research that could enable it to produce nuclear weapons, but was not attempting to do so.[\[216\]](#cite%5Fnote-LATimesFeb2013-218)
* As of 2021, [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe%5FBiden "Joe Biden")'s Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony%5FBlinken "Antony Blinken") did not rule out a military intervention to stop Iran from obtaining nuclear weapons.[\[172\]](#cite%5Fnote-Blinken-174)[\[217\]](#cite%5Fnote-219)
* In June 2023, an assessment by the U.S. [Director of National Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director%5Fof%5FNational%5FIntelligence "Director of National Intelligence") concluded that Iran was not developing nuclear weapons, though it was improving its nuclear capabilities, reporting that "Iran is not currently undertaking the key nuclear weapons-development activities that would be necessary to produce a testable nuclear device".[\[218\]](#cite%5Fnote-nei-20230714-220)[\[219\]](#cite%5Fnote-odni-202306-221)

### Other international responses

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=8%20%22Edit%20section:%20Other%20international%20responses%22)\]

#### United Nations

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=9%20%22Edit%20section:%20United%20Nations%22)\]

In 2009, the United Nations built a seismic monitoring station in Turkmenistan near its border with Iran, to detect tremors from nuclear explosions.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] The UN Security Council has demanded Iran freeze all forms of uranium enrichment.[\[35\]](#cite%5Fnote-UNSEC%5FRes-37) Iran has argued these demands unfairly compel it to abandon its rights under the Nuclear NonProliferation Treaty to peaceful nuclear technology for civilian energy purposes.[\[12\]](#cite%5Fnote-UNNews2-12)

On 29 December 2009, Zongo Saidou, a sanctions advisor for the U.N., said that as far as he knew, none of the U.N.'s member nations had alerted the sanctions committee about allegations of sales of uranium to Iran from Kazakhstan. "We don't have any official information yet regarding this kind of exchange between the two countries," Saidou said. "I don't have any information; I don't have any proof," Saidou said.[\[220\]](#cite%5Fnote-222) An intelligence report from an unknown country alleged that rogue employees of Kazakhstan were prepared to sell Iran 1,350 tons of purified uranium ore in violation of UN Security Council sanctions.[\[221\]](#cite%5Fnote-223) Russia said it had no knowledge of an alleged Iranian plan to import purified uranium ore from Kazakhstan. Kazakhstan denied the reports.[\[222\]](#cite%5Fnote-224) "Such fabrications of news are part of the psychological warfare (against Iran) to serve the political interests of the hegemonic powers," Iran said.[\[223\]](#cite%5Fnote-225) Askar Abdrahmanov, the official representative of the Ministry for Foreign Affairs of Kazakhstan, said "the references to the anonymous sources and unknown documents show groundlessness of these insinuations."[\[224\]](#cite%5Fnote-226)

#### China

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=10%20%22Edit%20section:%20China%22)\]

See also: [Nuclear weapons of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5Fweapons%5Fof%5FChina "Nuclear weapons of China")

The [Chinese Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry%5Fof%5FForeign%5FAffairs%5Fof%5Fthe%5FPeople's%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FChina "Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the People's Republic of China") supports the peaceful resolution of the Iran nuclear issue through diplomacy and negotiations. In May 2006 Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson Liu Jianchao stated "As a signatory to the Non-Proliferation Treaty, Iran enjoys the right to peaceful use of nuclear power, but it should also fulfil its corresponding responsibility and commitment". He added "It is urgently needed that Iran should fully cooperate with the IAEA and regain the confidence of the international community in its nuclear program".[\[225\]](#cite%5Fnote-eng20060531%5F269921-227)

In April 2008, several news agencies reported that China had supplied the IAEA with intelligence on Iran's nuclear program following a report by [Associated Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associated%5FPress "Associated Press") reporter George Jahn based on anonymous diplomatic sources.[\[180\]](#cite%5Fnote-autogenerated1-182) Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson [Jiang Yu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiang%5FYu "Jiang Yu") described these reports as "completely groundless and out of ulterior motives".[\[226\]](#cite%5Fnote-t421013-228)

In January 2010, China reiterated its calls for diplomatic efforts on the Iran nuclear issue over sanctions. "Dialogue and negotiations are the right ways of properly solving the Iran nuclear issue, and there is still room for diplomatic efforts," said Chinese spokesperson Jiang Yu. "We hope the relevant parties take more flexible and pragmatic measures and step up diplomatic efforts in a bid to resume talks as soon as possible," said Jiang.[\[227\]](#cite%5Fnote-229)

In September 2011 Israeli newspaper Haaretz reported several statements about Iran's nuclear program and China's foreign policy in the Middle East, made by independent Chinese expert on the Middle East who recently visited Israel at the invitation of "Signal", an organization that furthers academic ties between Israel and China. Yin Gang of the Chinese Academy of Social Sciences has expressed his opinion on China policies toward region, and according to Haaretz _he made surprising statement_: "China is opposed to any military action against Iran that would damage regional stability and interfere with the flow of oil. But China will not stop Israel if it decides to attack Iran. For all these reasons, Israel and the Middle East need a country like China. Israel needs China's power."[\[228\]](#cite%5Fnote-230)

In March 2012, Chinese foreign minister [Yang Jiechi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yang%5FJiechi "Yang Jiechi") said that "China is opposed to any country in the Middle East, including Iran, developing and possessing nuclear weapons.", adding that Iran nonetheless has the right to pursue nuclear activities for peaceful purposes.[\[229\]](#cite%5Fnote-231)

#### France

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=11%20%22Edit%20section:%20France%22)\]

See also: [France and weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "France and weapons of mass destruction")

On 16 February 2006 French Foreign Minister [Philippe Douste-Blazy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe%5FDouste-Blazy "Philippe Douste-Blazy") said "No civilian nuclear programme can explain the Iranian nuclear programme. It is a clandestine military nuclear programme."[\[230\]](#cite%5Fnote-cnn160206-232)

In January 2007, former French President [Jacques Chirac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques%5FChirac "Jacques Chirac"), speaking "off the record" to reporters from [The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FNew%5FYork%5FTimes "The New York Times"), indicated that if Iran possessed a nuclear weapon, the weapon could not be used. Chirac alluded to [mutually assured destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutually%5Fassured%5Fdestruction "Mutually assured destruction") when he stated:[\[231\]](#cite%5Fnote-nyt010207-233)

> "Where will it drop it, this bomb? On Israel? It would not have gone 200 meters into the atmosphere before Tehran would be razed."

#### Russia

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=12%20%22Edit%20section:%20Russia%22)\]

See also: [Russia and weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "Russia and weapons of mass destruction")

In 2005, Russian Advisor to Minister of Atomic Energy Lev Ryabev asserted that "neither the signing by Iran of the NPT, the adoption of the Additional Protocol (which provides for the right of inspection of any facility at any time with no prior notice), placement of nuclear facilities under IAEA safeguards, nor Russia's and Iran's commitments to repatriate spent nuclear fuel to Russia is seen as a good enough argument by the United States." Ryabev argued that "at the same time, such requirements are not imposed on, for example, Brazil, which has been developing its nuclear power industry and nuclear fuel cycle, including uranium enrichment."[\[232\]](#cite%5Fnote-234) The same year, during a meeting with [George W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George%5FW.%5FBush "George W. Bush"), [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir%5FPutin "Vladimir Putin") expressed concern about Iran’s nuclear proliferation believing that some uranium and centrifuge components used by Iran were from Pakistan.[\[233\]](#cite%5Fnote-235)

However, on 5 December 2007 Russian foreign minister [Sergey Lavrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey%5FLavrov "Sergey Lavrov") said he had seen no evidence of any nuclear weapons program in Iran, no matter how old.[\[234\]](#cite%5Fnote-times-20071205-236) On 16 October 2007 [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir%5FPutin "Vladimir Putin") visited [Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran "Tehran"), Iran to participate in the Second Caspian Summit, where he met with Iranian leader [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud%5FAhmadinejad "Mahmoud Ahmadinejad").[\[235\]](#cite%5Fnote-237) At a press conference after the summit Putin said that "Iran has the right to develop their peaceful nuclear programs without any restrictions".[\[236\]](#cite%5Fnote-238)

In 2009, Russian Major-General Pavel S. Zolotarev argued Iran's security could be partially be assured by supplying Iran with modern missile and air defense systems and offering for Iran to take part in the work of one of the data exchange centers in exchange for "concrete non-proliferation obligations".[\[237\]](#cite%5Fnote-239)

In May 2009, the [EastWest Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EastWest%5FInstitute "EastWest Institute") released a joint U.S.-Russian Threat Assessment on Iran's Nuclear and Missile Potential. The report concluded that there was "no IRBM/ICBM threat from Iran and that such a threat, even if it were to emerge, is not imminent." The report said there was no specific evidence that Iran was seeking the ability to attack Europe and that "it is indeed difficult to imagine the circumstances in which Iran would do so." The report said if Iran did pursue this capability, it would need six to eight years to develop a missile capable of carrying a 1,000 kilogram warhead 2,000 kilometers. The report said Iran ending "IAEA containment and surveillance of the nuclear material and all installed cascades at the Fuel Enrichment Plan" might serve as an early warning of Iranian intentions.[\[238\]](#cite%5Fnote-240)

Yevgeny Primakov, a former Russian prime minister considered the doyen of Moscow's Middle East experts, said he did "not believe that Iran had made a decision to acquire nuclear weapons. Russia has no concrete information that Iran is planning to construct a weapon. It may be more like Japan, which has nuclear readiness but does not have a bomb," Primakov said.[\[137\]](#cite%5Fnote-Russ09-139)

In February 2012, Russian Prime Minister Vladimir Putin said that Russia opposes Iran developing nuclear-weapons capability. "Russia is not interested in Iran becoming a nuclear power. It would lead to greater risks to international stability.", Putin said.[\[239\]](#cite%5Fnote-241)

#### United Kingdom

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=13%20%22Edit%20section:%20United%20Kingdom%22)\]

See also: [United Kingdom and weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FKingdom%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "United Kingdom and weapons of mass destruction")

The United Kingdom is part of the EU3+3 (UK, France, Germany, US, China and Russia) group of countries that are engaged in ongoing discussions with Iran.[\[240\]](#cite%5Fnote-242)The UK is therefore one of the countries that has stated that Iran would be provided with enriched fuel and support to develop a modern nuclear power program if it, in the words of the [Foreign Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign%5Fand%5FCommonwealth%5FOffice "Foreign and Commonwealth Office") spokesperson "_suspends all enrichment related activities, answer all the outstanding issues relating to Iran's nuclear programme and implement the additional protocol agreed with the IAEA_".[\[241\]](#cite%5Fnote-243)The UK (with China, France, Germany and Russia) put forward the three Security Council resolutions that have been passed in the UN.

On 8 May 2006, Former Deputy Commander-in-Chief of British Land Forces, General Sir Hugh Beach, former Cabinet Ministers, scientists and campaigners joined a delegation to Downing Street opposing military intervention in Iran. The delegation delivered two letters to Prime Minister Tony Blair from 1,800 physicists warning that the military intervention and the use of nuclear weapons would have disastrous consequences for the security of Britain and the rest of world. The letters carried the signatures of academics, politicians and scientists including some of 5 physicists who are Nobel Laureates.[CASMII delegation](http://www.payvand.com/news/06/may/1113.html) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20060516231537/http://www.payvand.com/news/06/may/1113.html) 16 May 2006 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback%5FMachine "Wayback Machine")

In 2006 [Boris Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris%5FJohnson "Boris Johnson"), then a member of Parliament, argued in his newspaper column that it was reasonable for Iran to seek nuclear weapons and that a nuclear-armed Iran could make the Middle East more stable.[\[242\]](#cite%5Fnote-Johnson%5F2006-244)[\[243\]](#cite%5Fnote-ES%5F2006%5FJohnson%5Fresponse-245) On assuming office he called for a new deal.[\[244\]](#cite%5Fnote-Johnson%5FBBC%5FJan%5F2020-246)

#### Israel

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=14%20%22Edit%20section:%20Israel%22)\]

See also: [Israel and weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "Israel and weapons of mass destruction")

Israel, which is not a party to the [Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5FNon-Proliferation%5FTreaty "Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty") and which is widely believed to possess [nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5Fweapon "Nuclear weapon"),[\[245\]](#cite%5Fnote-247) has frequently claimed that Iran is actively pursuing a nuclear weapons program.[\[246\]](#cite%5Fnote-248) Arguing an "existential threat from Iran", Israel has issued several veiled and explicit threats to attack Iran.[\[247\]](#cite%5Fnote-249)[\[248\]](#cite%5Fnote-250)[\[249\]](#cite%5Fnote-251) [Mike Mullen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike%5FMullen "Mike Mullen"), former chairman of the U.S. [Joint Chiefs of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint%5FChiefs%5Fof%5FStaff "Joint Chiefs of Staff"), has cautioned that an Israeli air attack on Iran would be high-risk and warned against Israel striking Iran.[\[250\]](#cite%5Fnote-252)

[George Friedman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George%5FFriedman "George Friedman"), head of the global intelligence company [Stratfor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stratfor "Stratfor"), has said\[_[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual%5Fof%5FStyle/Dates%5Fand%5Fnumbers#Chronological%5Fitems "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\] Iran is "decades away" from developing any credible nuclear-arms capacity and that an attack on Iran would have grave repercussions for the global economy.[\[61\]](#cite%5Fnote-online.barrons.com-63) If Iran ever did develop nuclear weapons, Israeli academic [Avner Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avner%5FCohen "Avner Cohen") said "that the prospect of a deliberate Iranian first nuclear strike on Israel, an out-of the-blue scenario, is virtually nonexistent... \[T\]he chances of Iran – or for that matter any other nuclear power – unleashing a nuclear strike against Israel, which has nuclear capabilities itself, strike me as close to zero."[\[251\]](#cite%5Fnote-Hatz%5F05Jun2006-253)

Walter Pincus of the _Washington Post_ has written that Israel's stance on nuclear arms complicates efforts against Iran.[\[252\]](#cite%5Fnote-254) [Gawdat Bahgat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gawdat%5FBahgat "Gawdat Bahgat") of the [National Defense University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National%5FDefense%5FUniversity%5F%28Washington,%5FD.C.%29 "National Defense University (Washington, D.C.)") believes Iran's nuclear program is partially formed on the potential threat of a nuclear Israel.[\[34\]](#cite%5Fnote-Gawdat%5FBahgat-36) Iran and the [Arab League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%5FLeague "Arab League") have proposed that the Middle East be established as a Nuclear Weapon Free Zone.[\[4\]](#cite%5Fnote-IranDAConf-4)[\[225\]](#cite%5Fnote-eng20060531%5F269921-227) Israel said in May 2010 it would not consider taking part in nuclear weapon-free zone discussions or joining the Nuclear Nonproliferation Treaty.[\[253\]](#cite%5Fnote-255) The UN Security Council has also pushed for a nuclear-weapon free zone in the Middle East, and has urged all countries to sign and adhere the 1970 Nuclear Nonproliferation Treaty.[\[254\]](#cite%5Fnote-256)

In May 2010, Israel reportedly deployed [Dolphin class submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolphin%5Fclass%5Fsubmarine "Dolphin class submarine") with nuclear missiles capable of reaching any target in Iran in the [Persian Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian%5FGulf "Persian Gulf"). Their reported missions were to deter Iran, gather intelligence, and to potentially land [Mossad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mossad "Mossad") agents on the Iranian coast.[\[255\]](#cite%5Fnote-257) In 2018, the Israeli prime minister said that the Mossad seized about one hundred thousand documents of Iran's nuclear program.[\[256\]](#cite%5Fnote-258)

### Netherlands

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=15%20%22Edit%20section:%20Netherlands%22)\]

According to a [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch%5Fpeople "Dutch people") newspaper, the Netherlands had launched an operation to infiltrate and sabotage the Iranian weapons industry, but ended the operation due to increasing fears of an American or Israeli attack on Iran's nuclear facilities.[\[257\]](#cite%5Fnote-259)

### Muslim countries

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=16%20%22Edit%20section:%20Muslim%20countries%22)\]

The [A.Q. Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul%5FQadeer%5FKhan "Abdul Qadeer Khan") network, established to procure equipment and material for Pakistan's [nuclear weapons program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "Pakistan and weapons of mass destruction") (gas-centrifuge-based programme), also supplied Iran with critical technology for its uranium enrichment program, and helped "put Iran on a fast track toward becoming a nuclear weapons power."[\[258\]](#cite%5Fnote-260)

The 2008 Annual [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab "Arab") Public Opinion Poll, Survey of the [Anwar Sadat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar%5FSadat "Anwar Sadat") Chair for Peace and Development at the [University of Maryland, College Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University%5Fof%5FMaryland,%5FCollege%5FPark "University of Maryland, College Park") conducted in Egypt, Jordan, [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon"), Morocco, [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi%5FArabia "Saudi Arabia") and the [UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAE "UAE") in March 2008 noted the following as a key finding.[\[259\]](#cite%5Fnote-0414%5Fmiddle%5Feast%5Ftelhami-261)

> "In contrast with the fears of many Arab governments, the Arab public does not appear to see Iran as a major threat. Most believe that Iran has the right to its nuclear program and do not support international pressure to force it to curtail its program. A plurality of Arabs (44%) believes that if Iran were to acquire nuclear weapons, the outcome would be more positive for the region than negative."

[Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia "Indonesia"), the [world's most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam%5Fby%5Fcountry "Islam by country") Muslim-majority nation and a non-permanent member of the [U.N. Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.N.%5FSecurity%5FCouncil "U.N. Security Council") abstained from a vote in March 2008 on a [U.N. resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN%5FSecurity%5FCouncil%5FResolution%5F1803 "UN Security Council Resolution 1803") to impose a third set of sanctions on Iran.[\[260\]](#cite%5Fnote-262) It was the only country out of the [10 non-permanent members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.N.%5FSecurity%5FCouncil#Non-permanent%5Fmembers "U.N. Security Council") to abstain.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Pakistan, which has [the second largest Muslim population in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam%5Fby%5Fcountry "Islam by country") is not a member of the [Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5FNon-Proliferation%5FTreaty "Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty") and already [possesses nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "Pakistan and weapons of mass destruction").

On 12 May 2006 AP published an interview with Pakistan's former [Chief of Army Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief%5Fof%5FArmy%5FStaff%5F%28Pakistan%29 "Chief of Army Staff (Pakistan)") of Pakistan Army General [Mirza Aslam Beg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirza%5FAslam%5FBeg "Mirza Aslam Beg")In the AP interview, Beg detailed nearly 20 years of Iranian approaches to obtain conventional arms and then technology for nuclear weapons. He described an Iranian visit in 1990, when he was Chief of Army Staff.

> _They didn't want the technology. They asked: 'Can we have a bomb?' My answer was: By all means you can have it but you must make it yourself. Nobody gave it to us._

Beg said he is sure Iran has had enough time to develop them. But he insists the Pakistani government didn't help, even though he says former Prime Minister Benazir Bhutto once told him the Iranians offered more than $4 billion for the technology.[\[261\]](#cite%5Fnote-263)[\[262\]](#cite%5Fnote-264)

In an article in 2005 about nuclear proliferation he stated

"_I would not like my future generations to live in the neighborhood of "[nuclear capable Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "Israel and weapons of mass destruction")."_"

"_Countries acquire the (nuclear) capability on their own, as we have done it. Iran will do the same, because they are threatened by Israel._"[\[263\]](#cite%5Fnote-265)

_[The San Francisco Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FSan%5FFrancisco%5FChronicle "The San Francisco Chronicle")_ reported on 31 October 2003, that Grand Ayatollahs, like [Ayatollah Yousef Sanei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah%5FYousef%5FSanei "Ayatollah Yousef Sanei"), and Iranian [clerics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clerics "Clerics") led by [Ayatollah Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah%5FAli%5FKhamenei "Ayatollah Ali Khamenei") have repeatedly declared that Islam forbids the development and use of all weapons of mass destruction. SFGate.com quoted Ayatollah Ali Khamenei as saying:

"_The Islamic Republic of Iran, based on its fundamental religious and legal beliefs, would never resort to the use of weapons of mass destruction. In contrast to the propaganda of our enemies, fundamentally we are against any production of weapons of mass destruction in any form._"[\[264\]](#cite%5Fnote-sfc31oct03-266)

On 21 April 2006, at a [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas") rally in [Damascus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damascus "Damascus"), Anwar Raja, the [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon")\-based representative of the [Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular%5FFront%5Ffor%5Fthe%5FLiberation%5Fof%5FPalestine "Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine"), a party that achieved 4.25% of the votes and holds 3 out the 132 seats in the [Palestinian Legislative Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian%5FLegislative%5FCouncil "Palestinian Legislative Council") following the [election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%5FPalestinian%5Flegislative%5Felection "2006 Palestinian legislative election") declared:

"_The Muslim, Iranian, fighting people now possess nuclear capabilities. My brother, the Iranian representative sitting here, let me tell you that we, the Palestinian people, are in favour of Iran having a nuclear bomb, not just energy for peaceful purposes._"[\[265\]](#cite%5Fnote-memri1114-267)

On 3 May 2006 Iraqi Shia cleric Ayatollah [Ahmad Husseini Al Baghdadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad%5FHusseini%5FAl%5FBaghdadi?action=edit&redlink=1 "Ahmad Husseini Al Baghdadi (page does not exist)"), who opposes the [presence of US forces in Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-invasion%5FIraq,%5F2003–present "Post-invasion Iraq, 2003–present") and is an advocate of violent [jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jihad "Jihad") was interviewed on Syrian TV. In his interview he said:[\[266\]](#cite%5Fnote-268)

"_How can they face Iran? How come Israel has 50 nuclear bombs? Why are they selective? Why shouldn't an Islamic or Arab country have a nuclear bomb? I am not referring to the Iranian program, which the Iranians say is for peaceful purposes. I am talking about a nuclear bomb._"

"_This Arab Islamic nation must obtain a nuclear bomb. Without a nuclear bomb, we will continue to be oppressed,_"

#### Baku declaration

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=17%20%22Edit%20section:%20Baku%20declaration%22)\]

A declaration signed on 20 June 2006 by the foreign ministers of 56 nations of the 57-member [Organisation of the Islamic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FConference "Organisation of the Islamic Conference") stated that "the only way to resolve Iran's nuclear issue is to resume negotiations without any preconditions and to enhance co-operation with the involvement of all relevant parties".

#### Qatar and Arab vote against the U.N. Security Council resolution

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=18%20%22Edit%20section:%20Qatar%20and%20Arab%20vote%20against%20the%20U.N.%20Security%20Council%20resolution%22)\]

31 July 2006: The UN Security Council gives until 31 August 2006 for Iran to suspend all uranium enrichment and related activities or face the prospect of sanctions.[\[267\]](#cite%5Fnote-269) The draft passed by a vote of 14–1 (Qatar, which represents Arab states on the council, opposing). The same day, Iran's U.N. Ambassador Javad Zarif qualified the resolution as "arbitrary" and illegal because the NTP protocol explicitly guarantees under international law Iran's right to pursue nuclear activities for peaceful purposes. In response to today's vote at the UN, Iranian President Mahmoud Ahmadinejad said that his country will revise his position vis-à-vis the economic/incentive package offered previously by the G-6 (5 permanent Security council members plus Germany.)[\[268\]](#cite%5Fnote-270)

In December 2006, the [Gulf Cooperation Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperation%5FCouncil%5Ffor%5Fthe%5FArab%5FStates%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGulf "Cooperation Council for the Arab States of the Gulf") called for a nuclear weapons free Middle East and recognition of the right of a country to expertise in the field of nuclear energy for peaceful purposes.[\[269\]](#cite%5Fnote-271)

### Non-Aligned Movement

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=19%20%22Edit%20section:%20Non-Aligned%20Movement%22)\]

The Non-Aligned Movement has said that the present situation whereby Nuclear Weapon States monopolise the right to possess nuclear weapons is "highly discriminatory", and they have pushed for steps to accelerate the process of nuclear disarmament.[\[149\]](#cite%5Fnote-autogenerated1998-151)

On 16 September 2006 in Havana, Cuba, all of the 118 [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned%5FMovement "Non-Aligned Movement") member countries, at the summit level, declared supporting Iran's nuclear program for civilian purposes in their final written statement.[\[270\]](#cite%5Fnote-272) That is a clear majority of the 192 countries comprising the entire United Nations, which comprise 55% of the [world population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World%5Fpopulation "World population").

On 11 September 2007 the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned%5FMovement "Non-Aligned Movement") rejected any "interference" in Iran's nuclear transparency deal with U.N. inspectors by Western countries through the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN%5FSecurity%5FCouncil "UN Security Council").[\[36\]](#cite%5Fnote-ReutersNAM-38)

On 30 July 2008 the Non-Aligned Movement welcomed the continuing cooperation of Iran with the IAEA and reaffirmed Iran's right to the peaceful uses of nuclear technology. The movement further called for the establishment of a nuclear weapons free zone in the Middle East and called for a comprehensive multilaterally negotiated instrument which prohibits threats of attacks on nuclear facilities devoted to peaceful uses of nuclear energy.[\[271\]](#cite%5Fnote-273)

## Biological weapons

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=20%20%22Edit%20section:%20Biological%20weapons%22)\]

Iran ratified the [Biological Weapons Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological%5FWeapons%5FConvention "Biological Weapons Convention") on 22 August 1973.[\[2\]](#cite%5Fnote-OPBW-2)

Iran has advanced biology and genetic engineering research programs supporting an industry that produces world-class vaccines for both domestic use and export.[\[272\]](#cite%5Fnote-vacc-274) The dual-use nature of these facilities means that Iran, like any country with advanced biological research programs, could easily produce biological warfare agents.

A 2005 report from the [United States Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5FDepartment%5Fof%5FState "United States Department of State") claimed that Iran began work on offensive biological weapons during the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War"), and that their large legitimate bio-technological and bio-medical industry "could easily hide pilot to industrial-scale production capabilities for a potential BW program, and could mask procurement of BW-related process equipment". The report further said that "available information about Iranian activities indicates a maturing offensive program with a rapidly evolving capability that may soon include the ability to deliver these weapons by a variety of means".[\[273\]](#cite%5Fnote-275)

According to the [Nuclear Threat Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5FThreat%5FInitiative "Nuclear Threat Initiative"), Iran is known to possess cultures of the many [biological agents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological%5Fagent "Biological agent") for legitimate scientific purposes which have been weaponised by other nations in the past, or could theoretically be weaponised. Although they do not allege that Iran has attempted to weaponise them, Iran possesses sufficient biological facilities to potentially do so.[\[274\]](#cite%5Fnote-bioref-276)

## Chemical weapons

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=21%20%22Edit%20section:%20Chemical%20weapons%22)\]

[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chemical%5Fweapon1.jpg)

Iranian soldier with [gas mask](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas%5Fmask "Gas mask") under [chemical bombardment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical%5Fwarfare "Chemical warfare") by Iraqi forces in the battlefield during the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War")

Iran has experienced attack by [chemical warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical%5Fwarfare "Chemical warfare") (CW) on the battlefield and suffered hundreds of thousands of casualties, both civilian and military, [in such attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi%5Fchemical%5Fattacks%5Fagainst%5FIran "Iraqi chemical attacks against Iran") during the 1980–88 [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War"). Iran was completely unprepared for chemical warfare and did not have sufficient gas masks for its troops. Due to sanctions, Iran had to purchase gas masks from North Korea or commercial painting respirator masks bought from the West. Iran is not known to have resorted to using chemical weapons in retaliation for Iraqi chemical weapons attacks during the Iran–Iraq War despite the fact it would have been legally entitled to do so under the then-existing international treaties on the use of chemical weapons which only prohibited the first use of such weapons.[\[275\]](#cite%5Fnote-277) Still Iran did develop a chemical-weapons-program during the latter part of that war, and in 1989, _[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FNew%5FYork%5FTimes "The New York Times")_ reported that Iran started a major campaign to produce and stockpile chemical weapons after a truce was agreed with Iraq.[\[276\]](#cite%5Fnote-278)

On 13 January 1993 Iran signed the [Chemical Weapons Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical%5FWeapons%5FConvention "Chemical Weapons Convention") (CWC) and ratified it on 3 November 1997\. In the official declaration submitted to the [Organisation for the Prohibition of Chemical Weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation%5Ffor%5Fthe%5FProhibition%5Fof%5FChemical%5FWeapons "Organisation for the Prohibition of Chemical Weapons") (OPCW), the Iranian government acknowledged that it had developed a chemical weapons program in the 1980s but asserted that it had since ceased the program and destroyed the stockpiles of operational weapons.[\[277\]](#cite%5Fnote-279)

In an interview with [Gareth Porter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gareth%5FPorter "Gareth Porter"), [Mohsen Rafighdoost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohsen%5FRafighdoost "Mohsen Rafighdoost"), the Minister of the [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") throughout the eight-year Iran-Iraq war, described how supreme leader [Ayatollah Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah%5FKhomeini "Ayatollah Khomeini") had twice blocked his proposal to begin working on both nuclear and chemical weapons to counter Iraqi chemical attacks, which Rafighdoost interpreted as a [fatwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatwa "Fatwa") against their use and production, because it was issued by the "[guardian jurist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guardianship%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FJurists "Guardianship of the Islamic Jurists")".[\[278\]](#cite%5Fnote-280)

A U.S. [Central Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central%5FIntelligence%5FAgency "Central Intelligence Agency") report dated January 2001 speculated that Iran had manufactured and stockpiled chemical weapons – including [blister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blister%5Fagent "Blister agent"), [blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood%5Fagent "Blood agent"), [choking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary%5Fagent "Pulmonary agent"), and probably [nerve agents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nerve%5Fagent "Nerve agent"), and the bombs and artillery shells to deliver them. It further claimed that during the first half of 2001, Iran continued to seek production technology, training, expertise, equipment, and chemicals from entities in Russia and China that could be used to help Iran reach its goal of having indigenous nerve agent production capability.[\[279\]](#cite%5Fnote-cia-report-281) However the certainty of this assessment declined and in 2007 the U.S. [Defense Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense%5FIntelligence%5FAgency "Defense Intelligence Agency") limited its public assessment to just noting that "Iran has a large and growing commercial chemical industry that could be used to support a chemical agent mobilization capability."[\[280\]](#cite%5Fnote-282)

Iran is a signatory of the Chemical Weapons Convention, which bans chemical weapons, delivery systems, and production facilities.[\[3\]](#cite%5Fnote-status-3) Iran has reiterated its commitment to the CWC and its full support for the work of the OPCW, in particular in view of the considerable suffering these weapons have caused to the Iranian people.[\[281\]](#cite%5Fnote-283) Iran has not made any declaration of a weapons stockpile under the treaty.[\[282\]](#cite%5Fnote-284)

In 2016 Iranian chemists synthesised five [Novichok nerve agents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novichok%5Fagents "Novichok agents"), originally developed in the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet%5FUnion "Soviet Union"), for analysis and produced detailed mass spectral data which was added to the OPCW Central Analytical Database.[\[283\]](#cite%5Fnote-spectroscopynow-20170101-285)[\[284\]](#cite%5Fnote-rcms-20161005-286) Previously there had been no detailed descriptions of their spectral properties in open scientific literature.[\[285\]](#cite%5Fnote-opcw-20130327-287)[\[283\]](#cite%5Fnote-spectroscopynow-20170101-285)

## Delivery systems

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=22%20%22Edit%20section:%20Delivery%20systems%22)\]

### Missiles

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=23%20%22Edit%20section:%20Missiles%22)\]

Main article: [Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps § Missile forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps#Missile%5Fforces "Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps")

A [Shahab-4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahab-4 "Shahab-4") with a range of 2,000 km and a payload of 1,000 kg is believed to be under development. Iran has stated the Shahab-3 is the last of its war missiles and the Shahab-4 is being developed to give the country the capability of launching communications and surveillance satellites. A [Shahab-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahab-5 "Shahab-5"), an [intercontinental ballistic missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercontinental%5Fballistic%5Fmissile "Intercontinental ballistic missile") with a 10,000 km range, has been alleged but not proven to be under development.[\[286\]](#cite%5Fnote-shahab-5-288)

In 2017, Iran tested the [Khorramshahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khorramshahr%5F%28missile%29 "Khorramshahr (missile)"), an [MRBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRBM "MRBM") that can carry an 1800 kg payload over 2000 km.[\[287\]](#cite%5Fnote-289)

Iran has 12 [X-55](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kh-55%5F%28missile%5Ffamily%29 "Kh-55 (missile family)") long range [cruise missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruise%5Fmissile "Cruise missile") purchased without nuclear warheads from [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine") in 2001\. The X-55 has a range of 2,500 to 3,000 kilometers.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Iran's most advanced missile, the [Fajr-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fajr-3%5Fballistic%5Fmissile "Fajr-3 ballistic missile"), has an unknown range but is estimated to be 2,500 km. The missile is radar evading and can strike targets simultaneously using multiple warheads.[\[288\]](#cite%5Fnote-290)

On 2 November 2006, Iran fired unarmed missiles to begin 10 days of military [war games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military%5Fsimulation "Military simulation"). Iranian state television reported "dozens of missiles were fired including [Shahab-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahab-2 "Shahab-2") and [Shahab-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahab-3 "Shahab-3") missiles. The missiles had ranges from 300 km to up to 2,000 km...Iranian experts have made some changes to Shahab-3 missiles installing cluster [warheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warhead "Warhead") in them with the capacity to carry 1,400 bombs." These launches come after some United States-led military exercises in the [Persian Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian%5FGulf "Persian Gulf") on 30 October 2006, meant to train for blocking the transport of [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction "Weapons of mass destruction").[\[289\]](#cite%5Fnote-291)

The Sejil is a two-stage, solid-propellant, surface-to-surface missile (SSM) produced by Iran with a reported 1,930 km (1,200 mi) range. A successful test launch took place on 12 November 2008.[\[290\]](#cite%5Fnote-292)

According to [Janes Information Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janes%5FInformation%5FServices "Janes Information Services"), details of the design other than the number of stages and that it uses solid fuel have not been released. Uzi Ruben, former director of Israel's Ballistic Missile Defense Organization, indicated that, "Unlike other Iranian missiles, the Sajil bears no resemblance to any North Korean, Russian, Chinese or Pakistani (missile technology). It demonstrates a significant leap in Iran's missile capabilities." Ruben went on to state that the Sejil-1 " ... places Iran in the realm of multiple-stage missiles, which means that they are on the way to having intercontinental ballistic missile (ICBM) capabilities ..."[\[291\]](#cite%5Fnote-293) As a weapon, the Sejil-1 presents much more challenge to Iran's potential enemies, as solid-fuel missiles can be launched with much less notice than liquid-fueled missiles, making them more difficult to strike prior to launch.[\[292\]](#cite%5Fnote-294)

Sejil-2 is an upgraded version of the Sejil. The Sejil-2 two-stage solid-fuel missile has a 2,000 km range and was first test fired on 20 May 2009.[\[293\]](#cite%5Fnote-295) The Sejil-2 surface-to-surface medium-range ballistic missile (MRBM) was first tested eight months prior to the actual test launch, which took place in the central Iranian province of Semnan.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Improvements include better navigation system, better targeting system, more payload, longer range, faster lift-off, longer storage time, quicker launch, and lower detection possibility.[\[294\]](#cite%5Fnote-296)

### Aircraft

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%5Fand%5Fweapons%5Fof%5Fmass%5Fdestruction&action=edit&section=24%20%22Edit%20section:%20Aircraft%22)\]

Main article: [Islamic Republic of Iran Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FAir%5FForce "Islamic Republic of Iran Air Force")

Any aircraft could potentially be used to host some form of WMD distribution system.\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Iran has a varied air force with aircraft purchased from many countries, including the United States. Due to sanctions, the Iranian government has encouraged the domestic production of aircraft and, since 2002, has built its own transport aircraft, fighters, and gunship helicopters.

> Strictly speaking, fuels enriched to 20% 235U are classified as HEU. Since many facilities with LEU cite a nominal enrichment of 20%, we have modified the definition of LEU to be £20% U for the purposes of RRSFDB. Since any fuel with exactly 20% enrichment before irradiation will have <20% enrichment after significant burnup, this does not violate the accepted definition.

* [Annotated Google map of Iranian nuclear sites](http://www.communitywalk.com/map/3078) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20070711081600/http://www.communitywalk.com/map/3078) 11 July 2007 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback%5FMachine "Wayback Machine")
* Report by the Director General of IAEA: _[Implementation of the NPT Safeguards Agreement and relevant provisions of Security Council resolutions 1737 (2006) and 1747 (2007) in the Islamic Republic of Iran](https://www.isis-online.org/publications/iran/IAEA%5FIran%5FReport%5F22Feb2008.pdf)_, 22 February 2008, GOV/2008/4.

Analysis

* [Towards Transatlantic Cooperation in Meeting the Iranian Nuclear Challenge](https://web.archive.org/web/20111208233740/http://ifri.org/downloads/Prolif%5FPaper%5FPerkovich.pdf) – analysis by George Perkovich, IFRI Proliferation Papers n°14, 2005
* [Iran's Nuclear History](http://www.payvand.com/news/03/oct/1015.html) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20190514125548/http://www.payvand.com/news/03/oct/1015.html) 14 May 2019 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback%5FMachine "Wayback Machine"), Prof. Mohammad Sahimi, Chairman of Chemical and Petroleum Engineering at the [University of Southern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University%5Fof%5FSouthern%5FCalifornia "University of Southern California"), and member of the [Union of Concerned Scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union%5Fof%5FConcerned%5FScientists "Union of Concerned Scientists"), 2 October 2003
* "Iran's Nuclear Fuel Cycle Facilities: A Pattern of Peaceful Intent?". `{{[cite web](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template:Cite%5Fweb "Template:Cite web")}}`: Missing or empty `|url=` ([help](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:CS1%5Ferrors#cite%5Fweb%5Furl "Help:CS1 errors")) (2.36 MB), [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US%5FState%5FDepartment "US State Department"), September 2005–presentation of US position. Satellite photography and quotes from Iranian leaders are documented and analyzed.
* [Iran as a Pioneer Case for Multilateral Nuclear Arrangements](https://mit.edu/stgs/irancrisis.html). Cambridge, Mass.: Massachusetts Institute of Technology.
* [Europe's Iran Policy: Breaking out of the Spiral of Mistrust](https://web.archive.org/web/20100502005400/http://en.qantara.de/webcom/show%5Farticle.php/%5Fc-476/%5Fnr-1320/i.html) by Prof. Volker Perthes, head of the German Institute for International and Security Affairs in Berlin
* [Iran needs nuclear energy, not weapons](https://archive.today/20121209182518/http://webcache.googleusercontent.com/search?q=cache:Wv7d%5FFdiMH0J:mondediplo.com/2005/11/02iran), [Le Monde diplomatique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le%5FMonde%5Fdiplomatique "Le Monde diplomatique"), November 2005 – questions whether Iran's nuclear program was really clandestine as commonly claimed.
* [Forced to Fuel](https://web.archive.org/web/20051213020138/http://hir.harvard.edu/articles/1294/) (Harvard Int'l Law Review, Vol. 26 No. 4 – Winter 2005) lays out the case for nuclear energy in Iran, by Prof. Muhammad Sahimi.
* [If Iran Gets Nukes](https://www.antiwar.com/orig/bayyenat.php?articleid=8401) by Abolghasem Bayyena, [Antiwar.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiwar.com "Antiwar.com"), 17 January 2006
